Key Concept: - Amazon Comprehend is a natural language processing (NLP) service that uses ML to find insights and relationships in text, including sentiment analysis. Why this is correct: - Use Amazon Comprehend to analyze sentiments of the chat conversations. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Use Amazon Rekognition to analyze sentiments of the chat conversations. — Amazon Rekognition is for image and video analysis, not for text-based sentiment analysis of chat conversations. - Train a Naive Bayes classifier to analyze sentiments of the chat conversations. — Training a custom Naive Bayes classifier requires data labeling, model training, and deployment — significantly more time than using Amazon Comprehend's pre-built sentiment analysis. - Use random forests to classify sentiments of the chat conversations. — Training a custom random forest model requires feature engineering, training, and deployment time. Amazon Comprehend provides ready-to-use sentiment analysis.
[MLA-C01 Q029] A company has a conversational AI assistant that sends requests through Amazon Bedrock to an Anthropic Claude large language model (LLM). Users report that when they ask similar questions multiple times, they sometimes receive different answers. An ML engineer needs to improve the responses to be more consistent and less random. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Increase the temperature parameter and the top_k parameter. B. Increase the temperature parameter. Decrease the top_k parameter. C. Decrease the temperature parameter. Increase the top_k parameter. D. Decrease the temperature parameter and the top_k parameter.	[Theo nguồn] D. Decrease the temperature parameter and the top_k parameter.. Key Concept: - Amazon Bedrock is a fully managed service that offers foundation models from leading AI companies through a unified API. Why this is correct: - Decrease the temperature parameter and the top_k parameter. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Increase the temperature parameter and the top_k parameter. — Increasing temperature makes outputs more random and diverse, which is the opposite of what's needed for consistent responses. - Increase the temperature parameter. Decrease the top_k parameter. — Increasing temperature makes outputs more random and diverse, which is the opposite of what's needed for consistent responses. - Decrease the temperature parameter. Increase the top_k parameter. — Increasing temperature makes outputs more random and diverse, which is the opposite of what's needed for consistent responses.
[MLA-C01 Q030] A company is using ML to predict the presence of a specific weed in a farmer's field. The company is using the Amazon SageMaker linear learner built-in algorithm with a value of multiclass_dassifier for the predictorjype hyperparameter. What should the company do to MINIMIZE false positives? A. Set the value of the weight decay hyperparameter to zero. B. Increase the number of training epochs. C. Increase the value of the target_precision hyperparameter. D. Change the value of the predictorjype hyperparameter to regressor.	[Theo nguồn] C. Increase the value of the target_precision hyperparameter.. Key Concept: - Precision measures the proportion of positive predictions that are correct. High precision means minimizing false positives. Why this is correct: - Increase the value of the target_precision hyperparameter. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Set the value of the weight decay hyperparameter to zero. — Setting weight decay to zero removes regularization, which may lead to overfitting but does not directly address false positive reduction. - Increase the number of training epochs. — Increasing epochs may improve overall accuracy but does not specifically target the reduction of false positives. - Change the value of the predictorjype hyperparameter to regressor. — Changing to regressor mode changes the task from classification to regression, which is inappropriate for a detection (classification) task.
[MLA-C01 Q031] A company has implemented a data ingestion pipeline for sales transactions from its ecommerce website. The company uses Amazon Data Firehose to ingest data into Amazon OpenSearch Service. The buffer interval of the Firehose stream is set for 60 seconds. An OpenSearch linear model generates real-time sales forecasts based on the data and presents the data in an OpenSearch dashboard. The company needs to optimize the data ingestion pipeline to support sub-second latency for the real-time dashboard. Which change to the architecture will meet these requirements? A. Use zero buffering in the Firehose stream. Tune the batch size that is used in the PutRecordBatch operation. B. Replace the Firehose stream with an AWS DataSync task. Configure the task with enhanced fan-out consumers. C. Increase the buffer interval of the Firehose stream from 60 seconds to 120 seconds. D. Replace the Firehose stream with an Amazon Simple Queue Service (Amazon SQS) queue.	[Theo nguồn] A. Use zero buffering in the Firehose stream. Tune the batch size that is used in the PutRecordBatch operation.. Key Concept: - Amazon Data Firehose delivers real-time streaming data to destinations. Zero buffering mode enables sub-second latency for real-time applications. Why this is correct: - Use zero buffering in the Firehose stream. Tune the batch size that is used in the PutRecordBatch operation. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Replace the Firehose stream with an AWS DataSync task. Configure the task with enhanced fan-out consumers. — AWS DataSync is designed for one-time or periodic data transfers, not for real-time streaming with sub-second latency. - Increase the buffer interval of the Firehose stream from 60 seconds to 120 seconds. — Increasing the buffer interval would increase latency, moving further away from the sub-second requirement. - Replace the Firehose stream with an Amazon Simple Queue Service (Amazon SQS) queue. — Replacing Firehose with SQS would require building a custom consumer and does not provide native integration for real-time dashboard updates.
[MLA-C01 Q032/Q261] A company has trained an ML model in Amazon SageMaker. The company needs to host the model to provide inferences in a production environment. The model must be highly available and must respond with minimum latency. The size of each request will be between 1 KB and 3 MB. The model will receive unpredictable bursts of requests during the day. The inferences must adapt proportionally to the changes in demand. How should the company deploy the model into production to meet these requirements? A. Create a SageMaker real-time inference endpoint. Configure auto scaling. Configure the endpoint to present the existing model. B. Deploy the model on an Amazon Elastic Container Service (Amazon ECS) cluster. Use ECS scheduled scaling that is based on the CPU of the ECS cluster. C. Install SageMaker Operator on an Amazon Elastic Kubernetes Service (Amazon EKS) cluster. Deploy the model in Amazon EKS. Set horizontal pod auto scaling to scale replicas based on the memory metric. D. Use Spot Instances with a Spot Fleet behind an Application Load Balancer (ALB) for inferences. Use the ALBRequestCountPerTarget metric as the metric for auto scaling.	[Theo nguồn] A. Create a SageMaker real-time inference endpoint. Configure auto scaling. Configure the endpoint to present the existing model.. Key Concept: - SageMaker endpoint auto scaling dynamically adjusts the number of instances based on demand, providing both cost efficiency and performance. Why this is correct: - Create a SageMaker real-time inference endpoint. Configure auto scaling. Configure the endpoint to present the existing model. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Deploy the model on an Amazon Elastic Container Service (Amazon ECS) cluster. Use ECS scheduled scaling that is based on the CPU of the ECS cluster. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario. - Install SageMaker Operator on an Amazon Elastic Kubernetes Service (Amazon EKS) cluster. Deploy the model in Amazon EKS. Set horizontal pod auto scaling to scale replicas based on the memory metric. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario. - Use Spot Instances with a Spot Fleet behind an Application Load Balancer (ALB) for inferences. Use the ALBRequestCountPerTarget metric as the metric for auto scaling. — Deploying multiple static copies behind a load balancer does not provide dynamic scaling that adjusts capacity based on demand.
[MLA-C01 Q033/Q260] An ML engineer needs to use an Amazon EMR cluster to process large volumes of data in batches. Any data loss is unacceptable. Which instance purchasing option will meet these requirements MOST cost-effectively? A. Run the primary node, core nodes, and task nodes on On-Demand Instances. B. Run the primary node, core nodes, and task nodes on Spot Instances. C. Run the primary node on an On-Demand Instance. Run the core nodes and task nodes on Spot Instances. D. Run the primary node and core nodes on On-Demand Instances. Run the task nodes on Spot Instances.	[Theo nguồn] D. Run the primary node and core nodes on On-Demand Instances. Run the task nodes on Spot Instances.. Key Concept: - Amazon EC2 Spot Instances offer unused EC2 capacity at up to 90% discount. Ideal for fault-tolerant, flexible workloads like batch processing. Why this is correct: - Run the primary node and core nodes on On-Demand Instances. Run the task nodes on Spot Instances. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Run the primary node, core nodes, and task nodes on On-Demand Instances. — On-Demand Instances are more expensive than Spot Instances. Since the workload is interruptible, Spot Instances provide the best cost savings (up to 90% discount). - Run the primary node, core nodes, and task nodes on Spot Instances. — Core nodes store HDFS data — using Spot Instances for core nodes risks data loss when instances are reclaimed. - Run the primary node on an On-Demand Instance. Run the core nodes and task nodes on Spot Instances. — On-Demand Instances are more expensive than Spot Instances. Since the workload is interruptible, Spot Instances provide the best cost savings (up to 90% discount).
[MLA-C01 Q034/Q259] A company wants to improve the sustainability of its ML operations. Which actions will reduce the energy usage and computational resources that are associated with the company's training jobs? (Choose two.) A. Use Amazon SageMaker Debugger to stop training jobs when non-converging conditions are detected. B. Use Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth for data labeling. C. Deploy models by using AWS Lambda functions. D. Use AWS Trainium instances for training. E. Use PyTorch or TensorFlow with the distributed training option.	[Theo nguồn] A. Use Amazon SageMaker Debugger to stop training jobs when non-converging conditions are detected. | D. Use AWS Trainium instances for training.. Key Concept: - Amazon SageMaker Debugger provides real-time monitoring of training jobs, with built-in rules to detect common issues like vanishing gradients and overfitting. Why this is correct: - Use Amazon SageMaker Debugger to stop training jobs when non-converging conditions are detected. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. - Use AWS Trainium instances for training. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Use Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth for data labeling. — Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth is for data labeling and does not directly reduce energy usage or computational resources in training. - Deploy models by using AWS Lambda functions. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario. - Use PyTorch or TensorFlow with the distributed training option. — Distributed training uses MORE computational resources. It improves speed but doesn't reduce energy consumption.
[MLA-C01 Q035] A company is planning to create several ML prediction models. The training data is stored in Amazon S3. The entire dataset is more than 5 ТВ in size and consists of CSV, JSON, Apache Parquet, and simple text files. The data must be processed in several consecutive steps. The steps include complex manipulations that can take hours to finish running. Some of the processing involves natural language processing (NLP) transformations. The entire process must be automated. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Process data at each step by using Amazon SageMaker Data Wrangler. Automate the process by using Data Wrangler jobs. B. Use Amazon SageMaker notebooks for each data processing step. Automate the process by using Amazon EventBridge. C. Process data at each step by using AWS Lambda functions. Automate the process by using AWS Step Functions and Amazon EventBridge. D. Use Amazon SageMaker Pipelines to create a pipeline of data processing steps. Automate the pipeline by using Amazon EventBridge.	[Theo nguồn] D. Use Amazon SageMaker Pipelines to create a pipeline of data processing steps. Automate the pipeline by using Amazon EventBridge.. Key Concept: - SageMaker Pipelines provides a CI/CD service for ML that supports approval steps, allowing manual or automated approval gates before model deployment. Why this is correct: - Use Amazon SageMaker Pipelines to create a pipeline of data processing steps. Automate the pipeline by using Amazon EventBridge. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Process data at each step by using Amazon SageMaker Data Wrangler. Automate the process by using Data Wrangler jobs. — Data Wrangler jobs handle individual steps but lack the orchestration capabilities needed to automate a complex multi-step pipeline with dependencies. - Use Amazon SageMaker notebooks for each data processing step. Automate the process by using Amazon EventBridge. — Notebooks are interactive tools not designed for automated production pipelines. - Process data at each step by using AWS Lambda functions. Automate the process by using AWS Step Functions and Amazon EventBridge. — Lambda has execution time limits (15 min) that cannot handle hours-long processing steps. SageMaker Pipelines natively supports long-running ML tasks.
[MLA-C01 Q036/Q258] An ML engineer needs to use AWS CloudFormation to create an ML model that an Amazon SageMaker endpoint will host. Which resource should the ML engineer declare in the CloudFormation template to meet this requirement? A. AWS::SageMaker::Model B. AWS::SageMaker::Endpoint C. AWS::SageMaker::NotebookInstance D. AWS::SageMaker::Pipeline	[Theo nguồn] A. AWS::SageMaker::Model. Key Concept: - AWS CloudFormation AWS::SageMaker::Model resource creates a model that can be deployed to a SageMaker endpoint for hosting predictions. Why this is correct: - AWS::SageMaker::Model — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - AWS::SageMaker::Endpoint — This resource creates the hosting endpoint but requires a Model resource to be defined first. - AWS::SageMaker::NotebookInstance — This resource creates a development environment, not a deployable model that an endpoint can host. - AWS::SageMaker::Pipeline — This resource defines an ML pipeline workflow, not a deployable model resource.
[MLA-C01 Q037/Q257] An advertising company uses AWS Lake Formation to manage a data lake. The data lake contains structured data and unstructured data. The company's ML engineers are assigned to specific advertisement campaigns. The ML engineers must interact with the data through Amazon Athena and by browsing the data directly in an Amazon S3 bucket. The ML engineers must have access to only the resources that are specific to their assigned advertisement campaigns. Which solution will meet these requirements in the MOST operationally efficient way? A. Configure IAM policies on an AWS Glue Data Catalog to restrict access to Athena based on the ML engineers' campaigns. B. Store users and campaign information in an Amazon DynamoDB table. Configure DynamoDB Streams to invoke an AWS Lambda function to update S3 bucket policies. C. Use Lake Formation to authorize AWS Glue to access the S3 bucket. Configure Lake Formation tags to map ML engineers to their campaigns. D. Configure S3 bucket policies to restrict access to the S3 bucket based on the ML engineers' campaigns.	[Theo nguồn] C. Use Lake Formation to authorize AWS Glue to access the S3 bucket. Configure Lake Formation tags to map ML engineers to their campaigns.. Key Concept: - AWS Lake Formation simplifies building, securing, and managing data lakes. It can aggregate data from multiple sources including S3 and on-premises databases. Why this is correct: - Use Lake Formation to authorize AWS Glue to access the S3 bucket. Configure Lake Formation tags to map ML engineers to their campaigns. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Configure IAM policies on an AWS Glue Data Catalog to restrict access to Athena based on the ML engineers' campaigns. — Amazon Athena is a serverless SQL query service and does not provide the specific capability required in this scenario. - Store users and campaign information in an Amazon DynamoDB table. Configure DynamoDB Streams to invoke an AWS Lambda function to update S3 bucket policies. — Amazon DynamoDB is a NoSQL database service not designed for data lake aggregation from multiple heterogeneous sources. - Configure S3 bucket policies to restrict access to the S3 bucket based on the ML engineers' campaigns. — Managing individual S3 bucket policies per user becomes unwieldy at scale and doesn't integrate with Lake Formation's built-in access control.
[MLA-C01 Q038] An ML engineer needs to use data with Amazon SageMaker Canvas to train an ML model. The data is stored in Amazon S3 and is complex in structure. The ML engineer must use a file format that minimizes processing time for the data. Which file format will meet these requirements? A. CSV files compressed with Snappy B. JSON objects in JSONL format C. JSON files compressed with gzip D. Apache Parquet files	[Theo nguồn] D. Apache Parquet files. Key Concept: - Apache Parquet is a columnar storage format optimized for analytics workloads. It provides efficient compression and encoding, minimizing processing time. Why this is correct: - Apache Parquet files — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - CSV files compressed with Snappy — CSV files are row-oriented and require full file scanning. Parquet's columnar format allows selective column reading, reducing processing time significantly. - JSON objects in JSONL format — JSON/JSONL files are text-based and row-oriented with parsing overhead. Parquet's binary columnar format is more efficient for analytical workloads. - JSON files compressed with gzip — JSON/JSONL files are text-based and row-oriented with parsing overhead. Parquet's binary columnar format is more efficient for analytical workloads.
[MLA-C01 Q039] An ML engineer is evaluating several ML models and must choose one model to use in production. The cost of false negative predictions by the models is much higher than the cost of false positive predictions. Which metric finding should the ML engineer prioritize the MOST when choosing the model? A. Low precision B. High precision C. Low recall D. High recall	[Theo nguồn] D. High recall. Key Concept: - Recall measures the proportion of actual positives correctly identified. High recall is critical when the cost of false negatives is high. Why this is correct: - High recall — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Low precision — Low precision means many false positives. The goal should be high recall while maintaining acceptable precision, not intentionally low precision. - High precision — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario. - Low recall — Low recall means missing many actual positive cases (false negatives), which is exactly what should be avoided when false negatives are costly.
[MLA-C01 Q040] A company has trained and deployed an ML model by using Amazon SageMaker. The company needs to implement a solution to record and monitor all the API call events for the SageMaker endpoint. The solution also must provide a notification when the number of API call events breaches a threshold. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Use SageMaker Debugger to track the inferences and to report metrics. Create a custom rule to provide a notification when the threshold is breached. B. Use SageMaker Debugger to track the inferences and to report metrics. Use the tensor_variance built-in rule to provide a notification when the threshold is breached. C. Log all the endpoint invocation API events by using AWS CloudTrail. Use an Amazon CloudWatch dashboard for monitoring. Set up a CloudWatch alarm to provide notification when the threshold is breached. D. Add the Invocations metric to an Amazon CloudWatch dashboard for monitoring. Set up a CloudWatch alarm to provide notification when the threshold is breached.	[Theo nguồn] C. Log all the endpoint invocation API events by using AWS CloudTrail. Use an Amazon CloudWatch dashboard for monitoring. Set up a CloudWatch alarm to provide notification when the threshold is breached.. Key Concept: - AWS CloudTrail records API calls for your account, providing a complete audit trail of actions taken on AWS resources. Why this is correct: - Log all the endpoint invocation API events by using AWS CloudTrail. Use an Amazon CloudWatch dashboard for monitoring. Set up a CloudWatch alarm to provide notification when the threshold is breached. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Use SageMaker Debugger to track the inferences and to report metrics. Create a custom rule to provide a notification when the threshold is breached. — SageMaker Debugger monitors training jobs and model internals, not endpoint API call events. CloudTrail is the proper service for API call auditing. - Use SageMaker Debugger to track the inferences and to report metrics. Use the tensor_variance built-in rule to provide a notification when the threshold is breached. — SageMaker Debugger monitors training jobs and model internals, not endpoint API call events. CloudTrail is the proper service for API call auditing. - Add the Invocations metric to an Amazon CloudWatch dashboard for monitoring. Set up a CloudWatch alarm to provide notification when the threshold is breached. — The Invocations metric only shows aggregate counts and doesn't record individual API call events with details.
[MLA-C01 Q041] A company has AWS Glue data processing jobs that are orchestrated by an AWS Glue workflow. The AWS Glue jobs can run on a schedule or can be launched manually. The company is developing pipelines in Amazon SageMaker Pipelines for ML model development. The pipelines will use the output of the AWS Glue jobs during the data processing phase of model development. An ML engineer needs to implement a solution that integrates the AWS Glue jobs with the pipelines. Which solution will meet these requirements with the LEAST operational overhead? A. Use AWS Step Functions for orchestration of the pipelines and the AWS Glue jobs. B. Use processing steps in SageMaker Pipelines. Configure inputs that point to the Amazon Resource Names (ARNs) of the AWS Glue jobs. C. Use Callback steps in SageMaker Pipelines to start the AWS Glue workflow and to stop the pipelines until the AWS Glue jobs finish running. D. Use Amazon EventBridge to invoke the pipelines and the AWS Glue jobs in the desired order.	[Theo nguồn] C. Use Callback steps in SageMaker Pipelines to start the AWS Glue workflow and to stop the pipelines until the AWS Glue jobs finish running.. Key Concept: - SageMaker Pipelines provides a CI/CD service for ML that supports approval steps, allowing manual or automated approval gates before model deployment. Why this is correct: - Use Callback steps in SageMaker Pipelines to start the AWS Glue workflow and to stop the pipelines until the AWS Glue jobs finish running. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Use AWS Step Functions for orchestration of the pipelines and the AWS Glue jobs. — AWS Step Functions would require rebuilding orchestration outside SageMaker Pipelines, adding operational overhead. - Use processing steps in SageMaker Pipelines. Configure inputs that point to the Amazon Resource Names (ARNs) of the AWS Glue jobs. — Processing steps run SageMaker processing jobs and cannot directly invoke external workflows or wait for their completion. - Use Amazon EventBridge to invoke the pipelines and the AWS Glue jobs in the desired order. — EventBridge can trigger events but cannot synchronize execution order or pause pipeline steps while waiting for external jobs to complete.
[MLA-C01 Q042/Q256] A company is using an Amazon Redshift database as its single data source. Some of the data is sensitive. A data scientist needs to use some of the sensitive data from the database. An ML engineer must give the data scientist access to the data without transforming the source data and without storing anonymized data in the database. Which solution will meet these requirements with the LEAST implementation effort? A. Configure dynamic data masking policies to control how sensitive data is shared with the data scientist at query time. B. Create a materialized view with masking logic on top of the database. Grant the necessary read permissions to the data scientist. C. Unload the Amazon Redshift data to Amazon S3. Use Amazon Athena to create schema-on-read with masking logic. Share the view with the data scientist. D. Unload the Amazon Redshift data to Amazon S3. Create an AWS Glue job to anonymize the data. Share the dataset with the data scientist.	[Theo nguồn] A. Configure dynamic data masking policies to control how sensitive data is shared with the data scientist at query time.. Key Concept: - Amazon Redshift dynamic data masking applies masking rules at query time without modifying source data or creating separate anonymized copies. Why this is correct: - Configure dynamic data masking policies to control how sensitive data is shared with the data scientist at query time. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Create a materialized view with masking logic on top of the database. Grant the necessary read permissions to the data scientist. — Materialized views store a physical copy of data, effectively creating stored anonymized data which violates the requirement. - Unload the Amazon Redshift data to Amazon S3. Use Amazon Athena to create schema-on-read with masking logic. Share the view with the data scientist. — Amazon Athena is a serverless SQL query service and does not provide the specific capability required in this scenario. - Unload the Amazon Redshift data to Amazon S3. Create an AWS Glue job to anonymize the data. Share the dataset with the data scientist. — Unloading data to S3 and creating separate masked copies requires more implementation effort and creates additional data copies.
[MLA-C01 Q043] An ML engineer is using a training job to fine-tune a deep learning model in Amazon SageMaker Studio. The ML engineer previously used the same pre-trained model with a similar dataset. The ML engineer expects vanishing gradient, underutilized GPU, and overfitting problems. The ML engineer needs to implement a solution to detect these issues and to react in predefined ways when the issues occur. The solution also must provide comprehensive real-time metrics during the training. Which solution will meet these requirements with the LEAST operational overhead? A. Use TensorBoard to monitor the training job. Publish the findings to an Amazon Simple Notification Service (Amazon SNS) topic. Create an AWS Lambda function to consume the findings and to initiate the predefined actions. B. Use Amazon CloudWatch default metrics to gain insights about the training job. Use the metrics to invoke an AWS Lambda function to initiate the predefined actions. C. Expand the metrics in Amazon CloudWatch to include the gradients in each training step. Use the metrics to invoke an AWS Lambda function to initiate the predefined actions. D. Use SageMaker Debugger built-in rules to monitor the training job. Configure the rules to initiate the predefined actions.	[Theo nguồn] D. Use SageMaker Debugger built-in rules to monitor the training job. Configure the rules to initiate the predefined actions.. Key Concept: - Amazon SageMaker Debugger provides real-time monitoring of training jobs, with built-in rules to detect common issues like vanishing gradients and overfitting. Why this is correct: - Use SageMaker Debugger built-in rules to monitor the training job. Configure the rules to initiate the predefined actions. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Use TensorBoard to monitor the training job. Publish the findings to an Amazon Simple Notification Service (Amazon SNS) topic. Create an AWS Lambda function to consume the findings and to initiate the predefined actions. — TensorBoard provides visualization but requires manual monitoring and custom integration for automated reactions, adding significant operational overhead. - Use Amazon CloudWatch default metrics to gain insights about the training job. Use the metrics to invoke an AWS Lambda function to initiate the predefined actions. — CloudWatch default metrics don't include detailed training metrics like gradients and GPU utilization that SageMaker Debugger provides. - Expand the metrics in Amazon CloudWatch to include the gradients in each training step. Use the metrics to invoke an AWS Lambda function to initiate the predefined actions. — Expanding CloudWatch to include training-specific metrics requires custom instrumentation and lacks built-in rules for detecting common training issues.
[MLA-C01 Q044] A credit card company has a fraud detection model in production on an Amazon SageMaker endpoint. The company develops a new version of the model. The company needs to assess the new model's performance by using live data and without affecting production end users. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Set up SageMaker Debugger and create a custom rule. B. Set up blue/green deployments with all-at-once traffic shifting. C. Set up blue/green deployments with canary traffic shifting. D. Set up shadow testing with a shadow variant of the new model.	[Theo nguồn] D. Set up shadow testing with a shadow variant of the new model.. Key Concept: - SageMaker shadow testing allows you to evaluate a new model variant using live production traffic without impacting end users. Why this is correct: - Set up shadow testing with a shadow variant of the new model. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Set up SageMaker Debugger and create a custom rule. — SageMaker Debugger monitors training metrics, not production inference performance. It cannot evaluate a new model with live production data. - Set up blue/green deployments with all-at-once traffic shifting. — Blue/green deployment routes actual production traffic to the new model, potentially affecting end users. Shadow testing duplicates traffic without impacting users. - Set up blue/green deployments with canary traffic shifting. — Blue/green deployment routes actual production traffic to the new model, potentially affecting end users. Shadow testing duplicates traffic without impacting users.
[MLA-C01 Q045] A company stores time-series data about user clicks in an Amazon S3 bucket. The raw data consists of millions of rows of user activity every day. ML engineers access the data to develop their ML models. The ML engineers need to generate daily reports and analyze click trends over the past 3 days by using Amazon Athena. The company must retain the data for 30 days before archiving the data. Which solution will provide the HIGHEST performance for data retrieval? A. Keep all the time-series data without partitioning in the S3 bucket. Manually move data that is older than 30 days to separate S3 buckets. B. Create AWS Lambda functions to copy the time-series data into separate S3 buckets. Apply S3 Lifecycle policies to archive data that is older than 30 days to S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval. C. Organize the time-series data into partitions by date prefix in the S3 bucket. Apply S3 Lifecycle policies to archive partitions that are older than 30 days to S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval. D. Put each day's time-series data into its own S3 bucket. Use S3 Lifecycle policies to archive S3 buckets that hold data that is older than 30 days to S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval.	[Theo nguồn] C. Organize the time-series data into partitions by date prefix in the S3 bucket. Apply S3 Lifecycle policies to archive partitions that are older than 30 days to S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval.. Key Concept: - Amazon Athena is a serverless query service that makes it easy to analyze data in Amazon S3 using standard SQL. Why this is correct: - Organize the time-series data into partitions by date prefix in the S3 bucket. Apply S3 Lifecycle policies to archive partitions that are older than 30 days to S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Keep all the time-series data without partitioning in the S3 bucket. Manually move data that is older than 30 days to separate S3 buckets. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario. - Create AWS Lambda functions to copy the time-series data into separate S3 buckets. Apply S3 Lifecycle policies to archive data that is older than 30 days to S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario. - Put each day's time-series data into its own S3 bucket. Use S3 Lifecycle policies to archive S3 buckets that hold data that is older than 30 days to S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario.
[MLA-C01 Q046] A company has deployed an ML model that detects fraudulent credit card transactions in real time in a banking application. The model uses Amazon SageMaker Asynchronous Inference. Consumers are reporting delays in receiving the inference results. An ML engineer needs to implement a solution to improve the inference performance. The solution also must provide a notification when a deviation in model quality occurs. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Use SageMaker real-time inference for inference. Use SageMaker Model Monitor for notifications about model quality. B. Use SageMaker batch transform for inference. Use SageMaker Model Monitor for notifications about model quality. C. Use SageMaker Serverless Inference for inference. Use SageMaker Inference Recommender for notifications about model quality. D. Keep using SageMaker Asynchronous Inference for inference. Use SageMaker Inference Recommender for notifications about model quality.	[Theo nguồn] A. Use SageMaker real-time inference for inference. Use SageMaker Model Monitor for notifications about model quality.. Key Concept: - Amazon SageMaker Model Monitor continuously monitors ML models in production and alerts when data or model quality deviates from baselines. Why this is correct: - Use SageMaker real-time inference for inference. Use SageMaker Model Monitor for notifications about model quality. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Use SageMaker batch transform for inference. Use SageMaker Model Monitor for notifications about model quality. — Batch transform processes data in bulk and is not suitable for real-time use cases where immediate results are needed. - Use SageMaker Serverless Inference for inference. Use SageMaker Inference Recommender for notifications about model quality. — Serverless Inference has cold start latency that may not meet strict low-latency requirements for real-time applications. - Keep using SageMaker Asynchronous Inference for inference. Use SageMaker Inference Recommender for notifications about model quality. — Asynchronous inference is designed for large payloads and long processing times, not for low-latency scenarios requiring immediate responses.
[MLA-C01 Q047/Q255] An ML engineer needs to implement a solution to host a trained ML model. The rate of requests to the model will be inconsistent throughout the day. The ML engineer needs a scalable solution that minimizes costs when the model is not in use. The solution also must maintain the model's capacity to respond to requests during times of peak usage. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Create AWS Lambda functions that have fixed concurrency to host the model. Configure the Lambda functions to automatically scale based on the number of requests to the model. B. Deploy the model on an Amazon Elastic Container Service (Amazon ECS) cluster that uses AWS Fargate. Set a static number of tasks to handle requests during times of peak usage. C. Deploy the model to an Amazon SageMaker endpoint. Deploy multiple copies of the model to the endpoint. Create an Application Load Balancer to route traffic between the different copies of the model at the endpoint. D. Deploy the model to an Amazon SageMaker endpoint. Create SageMaker endpoint auto scaling policies that are based on Amazon CloudWatch metrics to adjust the number of instances dynamically.	[Theo nguồn] D. Deploy the model to an Amazon SageMaker endpoint. Create SageMaker endpoint auto scaling policies that are based on Amazon CloudWatch metrics to adjust the number of instances dynamically.. Key Concept: - SageMaker endpoint auto scaling dynamically adjusts the number of instances based on demand, providing both cost efficiency and performance. Why this is correct: - Deploy the model to an Amazon SageMaker endpoint. Create SageMaker endpoint auto scaling policies that are based on Amazon CloudWatch metrics to adjust the number of instances dynamically. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Create AWS Lambda functions that have fixed concurrency to host the model. Configure the Lambda functions to automatically scale based on the number of requests to the model. — Lambda functions have execution time limits and cold starts, making them unsuitable for hosting ML models with variable demand patterns. - Deploy the model on an Amazon Elastic Container Service (Amazon ECS) cluster that uses AWS Fargate. Set a static number of tasks to handle requests during times of peak usage. — A static number of ECS tasks cannot dynamically respond to unpredictable demand bursts, leading to either over-provisioning or poor performance during peaks. - Deploy the model to an Amazon SageMaker endpoint. Deploy multiple copies of the model to the endpoint. Create an Application Load Balancer to route traffic between the different copies of the model at the endpoint. — Deploying multiple static copies behind a load balancer does not provide dynamic scaling that adjusts capacity based on demand.
[MLA-C01 Q048] A company uses Amazon SageMaker Studio to develop an ML model. The company has a single SageMaker Studio domain. An ML engineer needs to implement a solution that provides an automated alert when SageMaker compute costs reach a specific threshold. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Add resource tagging by editing the SageMaker user profile in the SageMaker domain. Configure AWS Cost Explorer to send an alert when the threshold is reached. B. Add resource tagging by editing the SageMaker user profile in the SageMaker domain. Configure AWS Budgets to send an alert when the threshold is reached. C. Add resource tagging by editing each user's IAM profile. Configure AWS Cost Explorer to send an alert when the threshold is reached. D. Add resource tagging by editing each user's IAM profile. Configure AWS Budgets to send an alert when the threshold is reached.	[Theo nguồn] B. Add resource tagging by editing the SageMaker user profile in the SageMaker domain. Configure AWS Budgets to send an alert when the threshold is reached.. Key Concept: - AWS Budgets lets you set custom cost and usage budgets and sends alerts when thresholds are exceeded. Why this is correct: - Add resource tagging by editing the SageMaker user profile in the SageMaker domain. Configure AWS Budgets to send an alert when the threshold is reached. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Add resource tagging by editing the SageMaker user profile in the SageMaker domain. Configure AWS Cost Explorer to send an alert when the threshold is reached. — AWS Cost Explorer is for visualizing and analyzing costs but does not provide automated alerting when thresholds are reached. - Add resource tagging by editing each user's IAM profile. Configure AWS Cost Explorer to send an alert when the threshold is reached. — AWS Cost Explorer is for visualizing and analyzing costs but does not provide automated alerting when thresholds are reached. - Add resource tagging by editing each user's IAM profile. Configure AWS Budgets to send an alert when the threshold is reached. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario.
[MLA-C01 Q049] A company uses Amazon SageMaker for its ML workloads. The company's ML engineer receives a 50 MB Apache Parquet data file to build a fraud detection model. The file includes several correlated columns that are not required. What should the ML engineer do to drop the unnecessary columns in the file with the LEAST effort? A. Download the file to a local workstation. Perform one-hot encoding by using a custom Python script. B. Create an Apache Spark job that uses a custom processing script on Amazon EMR. C. Create a SageMaker processing job by calling the SageMaker Python SDK. D. Create a data flow in SageMaker Data Wrangler. Configure a transform step.	[Theo nguồn] D. Create a data flow in SageMaker Data Wrangler. Configure a transform step.. Key Concept: - Amazon SageMaker Data Wrangler provides a visual interface for data preparation, including anomaly detection, data transformation, and visualization capabilities. Why this is correct: - Create a data flow in SageMaker Data Wrangler. Configure a transform step. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Download the file to a local workstation. Perform one-hot encoding by using a custom Python script. — Downloading to a local workstation and writing custom scripts requires more effort than using Data Wrangler's visual interface. - Create an Apache Spark job that uses a custom processing script on Amazon EMR. — Creating a Spark job on EMR is significantly more complex than using Data Wrangler's visual transform steps for simple column operations. - Create a SageMaker processing job by calling the SageMaker Python SDK. — A SageMaker processing job requires writing a custom script, which is more effort than using Data Wrangler's built-in visual transforms.
[MLA-C01 Q050] A company is creating an application that will recommend products for customers to purchase. The application will make API calls to Amazon Q Business. The company must ensure that responses from Amazon Q Business do not include the name of the company's main competitor. Which solution will meet this requirement? A. Configure the competitor's name as a blocked phrase in Amazon Q Business. B. Configure an Amazon Q Business retriever to exclude the competitor’s name. C. Configure an Amazon Kendra retriever for Amazon Q Business to build indexes that exclude the competitor's name. D. Configure document attribute boosting in Amazon Q Business to deprioritize the competitor's name.	[Theo nguồn] A. Configure the competitor's name as a blocked phrase in Amazon Q Business.. Key Concept: - This question tests your understanding of AWS ML services and best practices. The correct solution provides the optimal balance of functionality, operational efficiency, and cost-effectiveness for the given scenario. Why this is correct: - Configure the competitor's name as a blocked phrase in Amazon Q Business. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. [MLA-C01 Q082] A company has an Amazon S3 bucket that contains 1 ТВ of files from different sources. The S3 bucket contains the following file types in the same S3 folder: CSV, JSON, XLSX, and Apache Parquet. An ML engineer must implement a solution that uses AWS Glue DataBrew to process the data. The ML engineer also must store the final output in Amazon S3 so that AWS Glue can consume the output in the future. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Use DataBrew to process the existing S3 folder. Store the output in Apache Parquet format. B. Use DataBrew to process the existing S3 folder. Store the output in AWS Glue Parquet format. C. Separate the data into a different folder for each file type. Use DataBrew to process each folder individually. Store the output in Apache Parquet format. D. Separate the data into a different folder for each file type. Use DataBrew to process each folder individually. Store the output in AWS Glue Parquet format.	[Theo nguồn] C. Separate the data into a different folder for each file type. Use DataBrew to process each folder individually. Store the output in Apache Parquet format.. Key Concept: - AWS Glue is a serverless ETL service that makes it easy to prepare and load data for analytics and ML. Why this is correct: - Separate the data into a different folder for each file type. Use DataBrew to process each folder individually. Store the output in Apache Parquet format. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Use DataBrew to process the existing S3 folder. Store the output in Apache Parquet format. — AWS Glue DataBrew does not have built-in features specifically for oversampling minority classes in imbalanced datasets like SageMaker Data Wrangler does. - Use DataBrew to process the existing S3 folder. Store the output in AWS Glue Parquet format. — AWS Glue DataBrew does not have built-in features specifically for oversampling minority classes in imbalanced datasets like SageMaker Data Wrangler does. - Separate the data into a different folder for each file type. Use DataBrew to process each folder individually. Store the output in AWS Glue Parquet format. — AWS Glue DataBrew does not have built-in features specifically for oversampling minority classes in imbalanced datasets like SageMaker Data Wrangler does.

It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario. - Perplexity — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario.

[MLA-C01 Q096] A company needs to use Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) to supplement an open source large language model (LLM) that runs on Amazon Bedrock. [MLA-C01 Q118] A company needs to perform feature engineering, aggregation, and data preparation. [MLA-C01 Q119] A company is developing a new online application to gather information from customers. An ML engineer has developed a new ML model that will determine a score for each customer. The model will use the score to determine which product to display to the customer. The ML engineer needs to minimize response-time latency for the model. How should the ML engineer deploy the application in Amazon SageMaker to meet these requirements? A. Configure batch transform. B. Configure a real-time inference endpoint. C. Configure a serverless inference endpoint. D. Configure an asynchronous inference endpoint.	[Theo nguồn] B. Configure a real-time inference endpoint.. Key Concept: - SageMaker real-time inference endpoints provide synchronous predictions with low latency, ideal for applications requiring immediate responses. Why this is correct: - Configure a real-time inference endpoint. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Configure batch transform. — Batch transform processes data in bulk and is not suitable for real-time use cases where immediate results are needed. - Configure a serverless inference endpoint. — Serverless Inference has cold start latency that may not meet strict low-latency requirements for real-time applications. - Configure an asynchronous inference endpoint. — Asynchronous inference is designed for large payloads and long processing times, not for low-latency scenarios requiring immediate responses.
[MLA-C01 Q120] A company is using Amazon EMR. The company has a large dataset in Amazon S3 that needs to be ingested into Amazon SageMaker Feature Store. [MLA-C01 Q122/Q286] An ML engineer wants an Amazon SageMaker notebook to automatically stop running after 1 hour of idle time. How can the ML engineer accomplish this goal? A. [MLA-C01 Q126] A company is using an Amazon S3 bucket to collect data that will be used for ML workflows. The company needs to use AWS Glue DataBrew to clean and normalize the data. [MLA-C01 Q133] A company has several teams that have developed separate prediction models on their own laptops. [MLA-C01 Q134] A company is training a large language model (LLM) by using on-premises infrastructure. A live conversational engine uses the LLM to help customers find real-time insights in credit card data. An ML engineer must implement a solution to train and deploy the LLM on Amazon SageMaker. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Use SageMaker Training Compiler to train the LLM. Deploy the LLM by using SageMaker real-time inference. B. Use SageMaker with deep learning containers for large model inference to train the LLM. Deploy the LLM by using SageMaker real-time inference. C. Use SageMaker Notebook Jobs to train the LLM. Deploy the LLM by using SageMaker Asynchronous Inference. D. Use SageMaker Studio to train the LLM. Deploy the LLM by using SageMaker batch transform.	[Theo nguồn] B. Use SageMaker with deep learning containers for large model inference to train the LLM. Deploy the LLM by using SageMaker real-time inference.. Key Concept: - SageMaker real-time inference endpoints provide synchronous predictions with low latency, ideal for applications requiring immediate responses. Why this is correct: - Use SageMaker with deep learning containers for large model inference to train the LLM. Deploy the LLM by using SageMaker real-time inference. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Use SageMaker Training Compiler to train the LLM. Deploy the LLM by using SageMaker real-time inference. — SageMaker Training Compiler optimizes model code to run faster on GPU instances but does not reduce infrastructure startup times between consecutive jobs. - Use SageMaker Notebook Jobs to train the LLM. Deploy the LLM by using SageMaker Asynchronous Inference. — Asynchronous inference is designed for large payloads and long processing times, not for low-latency scenarios requiring immediate responses. - Use SageMaker Studio to train the LLM. Deploy the LLM by using SageMaker batch transform. — Batch transform processes data in bulk and is not suitable for real-time use cases where immediate results are needed.
[MLA-C01 Q135] A company has an existing Amazon SageMaker model (v1) on a production endpoint. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Create a second production variant for v2. Assign 1% of the traffic to v2 and 99% of the traffic to v1. Collect all the output of v2 in an Amazon S3 bucket. If v2 performs as expected, switch all the traffic to v2. B. Create a second production variant for v2. Collect all the output in an Amazon S3 bucket. If v2 performs as expected, promote v2 to production.	[Theo nguồn] D. Deploy v2 into a shadow variant that samples 100% of the inference requests. If v2 performs as expected, promote v2 to production. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Create a second production variant for v2. Assign 1% of the traffic to v2 and 99% of the traffic to v1. Collect all the output of v2 in an Amazon S3 bucket. If v2 performs as expected, switch all the traffic to v2. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario. - Create a second production variant for v2. Assign 10% of the traffic to v2 and 90% of the traffic to v1. Collect all the output of v2 in an Amazon S3 bucket. If v2 performs as expected, switch all the traffic to v2. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. Turn on data capturing for the production endpoint. Write a script to pass 100% of input data to v2. [MLA-C01 Q137/Q223] An ML engineer is training an ML model to identify people’s health risk based on 20 features and 1 target. The target class has two values:• Likely to have health risk (positive class)• Unlikely to have health risk (negative class)The age range of people in the dataset is 30 years old to 60 years old. Age is one of the features. The ML engineer analyzes the features. For the positive class, the difference in proportions of labels (DPL) value is (+0.9) for the age range of 40 to 45 compared with all other age ranges. What should the ML engineer do to correct this data imbalance? A. Oversample the positive class for the age range of 40 to 45. B. Undersample the positive class for the age range of 40 to 45. C. Undersample the positive class for all age ranges except 40 to 45. D. Oversample the negative class for all age ranges except 40 to 45.	[Cần xác minh] Chưa chốt đáp án. Nguồn Q137 chọn D; Q223 chọn C, tương ứng B trong thứ tự Q137. AWS định nghĩa DPL = q_a − q_d. Nếu nhóm 40–45 là facet a, tỷ lệ nhãn dương của nhóm này cao hơn; giảm mẫu dương của nhóm này (B) làm giảm chênh lệch. Đề không chỉ rõ facet a/d. Giữ câu ở trạng thái cần xác minh, không tính điểm, thay vì chọn khóa nguồn có mâu thuẫn.

[MLA-C01 Q141] A company has significantly increased the amount of data that is stored as .csv files in an Amazon S3 bucket. [MLA-C01 Q151] An ML engineer is building an ML pipeline. The pipeline must process a dataset in two ways by using Amazon Athena. The pipeline must use batch processing to perform large-scale data transformations and for model training. The pipeline must also use near real-time processing to perform low-latency queries for inference and analytics. Which file format will provide the LEAST latency for both types of processing? A. CSV B. Apache Parquet C. Nested JSON D. Deserialized JSON	[Theo nguồn] B. Apache Parquet. Key Concept: - Amazon Athena is a serverless query service that makes it easy to analyze data in Amazon S3 using standard SQL. Why this is correct: - Apache Parquet — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - CSV — CSV files are row-oriented and require full file scanning. Parquet's columnar format allows selective column reading, reducing processing time significantly. - Nested JSON — JSON/JSONL files are text-based and row-oriented with parsing overhead. Parquet's binary columnar format is more efficient for analytical workloads. - Deserialized JSON — JSON/JSONL files are text-based and row-oriented with parsing overhead. Parquet's binary columnar format is more efficient for analytical workloads.

[MLA-C01 Q179] An ML engineer is training a text generation model on Amazon SageMaker AI. After several epochs, the loss function does not converge, and the model’s accuracy on the validation dataset starts to show oscillating results. The ML engineer needs to ensure that the model achieves generalization. Which solution will meet this requirement? A. Increase the learning rate and decrease the mini-batch size. B. Increase the learning rate as the number of epochs increases. C. Decrease the learning rate and increase the mini-batch size. D. Decrease the learning rate and decrease the mini-batch size.	[Theo nguồn] C. Decrease the learning rate and increase the mini-batch size.. Key Concept: - The learning rate controls the step size in gradient descent. A high learning rate causes oscillation, while a lower rate provides more stable convergence. Why this is correct: - Decrease the learning rate and increase the mini-batch size. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Increase the learning rate and decrease the mini-batch size. — Increasing the learning rate would worsen the oscillation problem. The oscillating pattern is a clear sign that the current learning rate is already too high. - Increase the learning rate as the number of epochs increases. — Increasing the learning rate would worsen the oscillation problem. The oscillating pattern is a clear sign that the current learning rate is already too high. - Decrease the learning rate and decrease the mini-batch size. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario.

[MLA-C01 Q181] A company needs to analyze a large dataset that is stored in Amazon S3 in Apache Parquet format. The company wants to use one-hot encoding for some of the columns. The company needs a no-code solution to transform the data. The solution must store the transformed data back to the same S3 bucket for model training. Which solution will meet these requirements? A. Configure an AWS Glue DataBrew project that connects to the data. Use the DataBrew interactive interface to create a recipe that performs the one-hot encoding transformation. Create a job to apply the transformation and to write the output back to an S3 bucket. B. Configure an AWS Glue Data Catalog table that points to the data. Use Amazon Athena to write SQL commands to perform the one-hot encoding transformation. Configure Athena to write the query results back to an S3 bucket. C. Configure an AWS Glue Data Catalog table that points to the data. Create an AWS Glue ETL interactive notebook. Use the notebook to perform the one-hot encoding transformation. Run the configured cells and write the results back to an S3 bucket. D. Configure an Amazon Redshift cluster to access the data by using Redshift Spectrum. Use SQL commands to perform the one-hot encoding transformation within Amazon Redshift. Configure Amazon Redshift to write the results back to an S3 bucket.	[Theo nguồn] A. Configure an AWS Glue DataBrew project that connects to the data. Use the DataBrew interactive interface to create a recipe that performs the one-hot encoding transformation. Create a job to apply the transformation and to write the output back to an S3 bucket.. Key Concept: - One-hot encoding converts categorical variables into a binary matrix representation, which is essential for neural networks that require numerical inputs. Why this is correct: - Configure an AWS Glue DataBrew project that connects to the data. Use the DataBrew interactive interface to create a recipe that performs the one-hot encoding transformation. Create a job to apply the transformation and to write the output back to an S3 bucket. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Configure an AWS Glue Data Catalog table that points to the data. Use Amazon Athena to write SQL commands to perform the one-hot encoding transformation. Configure Athena to write the query results back to an S3 bucket. — Amazon Athena is a serverless SQL query service and does not provide the specific capability required in this scenario. - Configure an AWS Glue Data Catalog table that points to the data. Create an AWS Glue ETL interactive notebook. Use the notebook to perform the one-hot encoding transformation. Run the configured cells and write the results back to an S3 bucket. — This option does not meet the requirements as effectively as the correct answer. It either adds unnecessary complexity, doesn't address the specific requirement, or introduces limitations that make it suboptimal for this scenario. - Configure an Amazon Redshift cluster to access the data by using Redshift Spectrum. Use SQL commands to perform the one-hot encoding transformation within Amazon Redshift. Configure Amazon Redshift to write the results back to an S3 bucket. — Amazon Redshift Spectrum can query S3 data but requires a Redshift cluster and does not provide automatic anomaly detection capabilities.

[MLA-C01 Q187] A travel company wants to create an ML model to recommend the next airport destination for its users. [MLA-C01 Q192] A healthcare company uses an Amazon SageMaker AI endpoint to host a model that predicts patient readmission risk to hospitals. During the month of testing, the shadow variant has a higher recall than the existing model but has a lower precision. What should the company do next? A. Promote the shadow variant to full production. B. Extend the shadow testing period to capture more data. Monitor the new model to determine whether precision improves. C. Use a blue/green deployment strategy to allocate a small percentage of traffic to the shadow variant to reduce model errors. D. Disable the shadow variant and roll back to the main variant.	[Theo nguồn] A. Promote the shadow variant to full production.. Key Concept: - SageMaker shadow testing allows you to evaluate a new model variant using live production traffic without impacting end users. Why this is correct: - Promote the shadow variant to full production. — This is correct because it directly addresses the requirement with the least operational overhead and is purpose-built for this use case. Why other options are incorrect: - Extend the shadow testing period to capture more data. Explanation: - - Anomaly/abnormal data points → RCF (Random Cut Forest) - Pixel-level image classification → Semantic Segmentation - Text summarization / translation / sequence output → seq2seq - RCF = unsupervised anomaly detection, works well with streaming data - Semantic Segmentation ≠ Object Detection (Object Detection dùng bounding boxes; Segmentation = per-pixel labels)
[MLA-C01 Q224] HOTSPOT - A company stores historical data in .csv files in Amazon S3. Only some of the rows and columns in the .csv files are populated. The columns are not labeled. An ML engineer needs to prepare and store the data so that the company can use the data to train ML models. Select and order the correct steps from the following list to perform this task. Each step should be selected one time or not at all. (Select and order three.) 1. Create an Amazon SageMaker batch transform job for data cleaning and feature engineering. 2. Store the resulting data back in Amazon S3. 3. Use Amazon Athena to infer the schemas and available columns. 4. Use AWS Glue crawlers to infer the schemas and available columns. 5. Use AWS Glue DataBrew for data cleaning and feature engineering. A. Step 1: Use AWS Glue crawlers to infer the schemas and available columns. Step 2: Use Amazon Athena to infer the schemas and available columns. Step 3: Create an Amazon SageMaker batch transform job for data cleaning and feature engineering. B. Step 1: Use AWS Glue crawlers to infer the schemas and available columns. Step 2: Use AWS Glue DataBrew for data cleaning and feature engineering. Step 3: Use Amazon Athena to infer the schemas and available columns. C. Step 1: Use AWS Glue crawlers to infer the schemas and available columns. Step 2: Create an Amazon SageMaker batch transform job for data cleaning and feature engineering. Step 3: Store the resulting data back in Amazon S3. D. Step 1: Use AWS Glue crawlers to infer the schemas and available columns. Step 2: Store the resulting data back in Amazon S3. Step 3: Use Amazon Athena to infer the schemas and available columns. E. Step 1: Use AWS Glue crawlers to infer the schemas and available columns. Step 2: Use AWS Glue DataBrew for data cleaning and feature engineering. Step 3: Store the resulting data back in Amazon S3. F. Step 1: Create an Amazon SageMaker batch transform job for data cleaning and feature engineering. Step 2: Use AWS Glue DataBrew for data cleaning and feature engineering. Step 3: Store the resulting data back in Amazon S3. [Có hình: xem bản Markdown tổng hợp.]	[Theo nguồn] E. Step 1: Use AWS Glue crawlers to infer the schemas and available columns. Step 2: Use AWS Glue DataBrew for data cleaning and feature engineering. Step 3: Store the resulting data back in Amazon S3.. Explanation: - - Khi cần khám phá và tạo schema cho dữ liệu chưa được định nghĩa trong một kho dữ liệu (data lake), hãy sử dụng AWS Glue crawlers và Data Catalog. - Khi cần một công cụ trực quan và dễ sử dụng để làm sạch và tiền xử lý dữ liệu, AWS Glue DataBrew là lựa chọn phù hợp. - Luôn lưu trữ dữ liệu được chuẩn bị cho ML trong Amazon S3 để các dịch vụ khác có thể dễ dàng truy cập.
[MLA-C01 Q225] An ML engineer needs to use Amazon SageMaker Feature Store to create and manage features to train a model. Select and order the steps from the following list to create and use the features in Feature Store. Each step should be selected one time. (Select and order three.) • Access the store to build datasets for training. • Create a feature group. • Ingest the records. A. Step 1: Access the store to build datasets for training. Step 2: Ingest the records. Step 3: Create a feature group. B. Step 1: Create a feature group. Step 2: Access the store to build datasets for training. Step 3: Ingest the records. C. Step 1: Ingest the records. Step 2: Create a feature group. Step 3: Access the store to build datasets for training. D. Step 1: Create a feature group. Step 2: Ingest the records. Step 3: Access the store to build datasets for training. [Có hình: xem bản Markdown tổng hợp.]	[Theo nguồn] D. Step 1: Create a feature group. Step 2: Ingest the records. Step 3: Access the store to build datasets for training.. Explanation: - - Các bước làm việc với SageMaker Feature Store có thể tóm tắt như sau: Define -> Ingest -> Use.
[MLA-C01 Q226] HOTSPOT - A company wants to host an ML model on Amazon SageMaker. An ML engineer is configuring a continuous integration and continuous delivery (Cl/CD) pipeline in AWS CodePipeline to deploy the model. The pipeline must run automatically when new training data for the model is uploaded to an Amazon S3 bucket. Select and order the pipeline's correct steps from the following list. Each step should be selected one time or not at all. (Select and order three.) 1. An S3 event notification invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. 2. S3 Lifecycle rule invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. 3. SageMaker retrains the model by using the data in the S3 bucket. 4. The pipeline deploys the model to a SageMaker endpoint. 5. The pipeline deploys the model to SageMaker Model Registry. A. Step 1: The pipeline deploys the model to SageMaker Model Registry. Step 2: An S3 lifecycle rule invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. Step 3: SageMaker retrains the model by using the data in the S3 bucket. B. Step 1: An S3 event notification invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. Step 2: S3 Lifecycle rule invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. Step 3: The pipeline deploys the model to a SageMaker endpoint. C. Step 1: An S3 event notification invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. Step 2: SageMaker retrains the model by using the data in the S3 bucket. Step 3: The pipeline deploys the model to SageMaker Model Registry. D. Step 1: An S3 event notification invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. Step 2: The pipeline deploys the model to a SageMaker endpoint. Step 3: The pipeline deploys the model to SageMaker Model Registry. E. Step 1: An S3 event notification invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. Step 2: The pipeline deploys the model to a SageMaker endpoint. Step 3: SageMaker retrains the model by using the data in the S3 bucket. F. Step 1: An S3 event notification invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. Step 2: SageMaker retrains the model by using the data in the S3 bucket. Step 3: The pipeline deploys the model to a SageMaker endpoint. [Có hình: xem bản Markdown tổng hợp.]	[Theo nguồn] F. Step 1: An S3 event notification invokes the pipeline when new data is uploaded. Step 2: SageMaker retrains the model by using the data in the S3 bucket. Step 3: The pipeline deploys the model to a SageMaker endpoint.. Explanation: - - Các pipeline CI/CD tự động hóa trong AWS thường bắt đầu bằng một sự kiện, chẳng hạn như một thông báo S3, một cam kết mã nguồn trong CodeCommit, hoặc một push vào ECR. - Hiểu rõ vai trò của từng dịch vụ: S3 (lưu trữ dữ liệu), SageMaker (huấn luyện và triển khai), CodePipeline (điều phối các bước trong pipeline), và S3 Event Notifications (kích hoạt pipeline).

Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) 3. Temperature 4. Token A. Explanation: - - Các thuật ngữ trong AI tạo sinh thường liên quan đến quy trình từ đầu đến cuối: Token là đơn vị xử lý cơ bản, Embedding là cách mô hình hiểu ngữ nghĩa của chúng, và RAG là một kỹ thuật để cải thiện phản hồi.
[MLA-C01 Q229] HOTSPOT - An ML engineer is working on an ML model to predict the prices of similarly sized homes. The model will base predictions on several features The ML engineer will use the following feature engineering techniques to estimate the prices of the homes: 1. Feature splitting 2. Logarithmic transformation 3. One-hot encoding 4. Standardized distribution Select the correct feature engineering techniques for the following list of features. Each feature engineering technique should be selected one time or not at all (Select three.) A. City (name): Feature splitting Type_year (type of home and year the home was built): Standardized distribution Size of the building (square feet or square meters): Logarithmic transformation B. City (name): One-hot encoding Type_year (type of home and year the home was built): Feature splitting Size of the building (square feet or square meters): Logarithmic transformation C. City (name): One-hot encoding Type_year (type of home and year the home was built): Standardized distribution Size of the building (square feet or square meters): Feature splitting D. City (name): Logarithmic transformation Type_year (type of home and year the home was built): Feature splitting Size of the building (square feet or square meters): One-hot encoding [Có hình: xem bản Markdown tổng hợp.]	[Theo nguồn] B. City (name): One-hot encoding Type_year (type of home and year the home was built): Feature splitting Size of the building (square feet or square meters): Logarithmic transformation. Explanation: - - Dữ liệu danh nghĩa (Categorical data): One-hot encoding, Label encoding. - Dữ liệu số bị lệch (Skewed numerical data): Logarithmic transformation, Square root transformation. - Dữ liệu phức tạp: Feature splitting.
[MLA-C01 Q230] A hospital wants to predict patient outcomes for the coming year. An ML engineer must improve several existing ML models that currently perform poorly. Select the correct regularization method from the following list to improve each model. Select each regularization method one time, more than one time, or not at all. (Choose three.) • L1 regularization • L2 regularization • Early stopping A. A linear regression whose coefficients should shrink but not become zero: L1 regularization A polynomial regression with irrelevant polynomial terms that should be eliminated: L2 regularization A logistic regression with highly correlated features to eliminate redundant predictors: L1 regularization B. A linear regression whose coefficients should shrink but not become zero: L2 regularization A polynomial regression with irrelevant polynomial terms that should be eliminated: L1 regularization A logistic regression with highly correlated features to eliminate redundant predictors: L2 regularization C. A linear regression whose coefficients should shrink but not become zero: L2 regularization A polynomial regression with irrelevant polynomial terms that should be eliminated: L1 regularization A logistic regression with highly correlated features to eliminate redundant predictors: L1 regularization D. A linear regression whose coefficients should shrink but not become zero: L2 regularization A polynomial regression with irrelevant polynomial terms that should be eliminated: L2 regularization A logistic regression with highly correlated features to eliminate redundant predictors: L1 regularization [Có hình: xem bản Markdown tổng hợp.]	[Theo nguồn] C. A linear regression whose coefficients should shrink but not become zero: L2 regularization A polynomial regression with irrelevant polynomial terms that should be eliminated: L1 regularization A logistic regression with highly correlated features to eliminate redundant predictors: L1 regularization. Explanation: - - Shrink but NOT zero → L2 (Ridge) - Eliminate / set to zero / feature selection → L1 (Lasso) - Stop training early to prevent overfitting → Early stopping - Correlated features + eliminate redundant → L1 (chọn đại diện, loại còn lại)
